<h3 id="additional-resources" style="color: rgb(0, 0, 0); font-family: 'Times New Roman'; font-style: normal; font-variant-ligatures: normal; font-variant-caps: normal; letter-spacing: normal; orphans: 2; text-align: start; text-indent: 0px; text-transform: none; widows: 2; word-spacing: 0px; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px; white-space: normal; text-decoration-thickness: initial; text-decoration-style: initial; text-decoration-color: initial;">Additional Resources</h3>
<ul style="color: rgb(0, 0, 0); font-family: 'Times New Roman'; font-size: medium; font-style: normal; font-variant-ligatures: normal; font-variant-caps: normal; font-weight: 400; letter-spacing: normal; orphans: 2; text-align: start; text-indent: 0px; text-transform: none; widows: 2; word-spacing: 0px; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px; white-space: normal; text-decoration-thickness: initial; text-decoration-style: initial; text-decoration-color: initial;">
<li>Create a team reference document listing all measurement renderings</li>
<li>Maintain conversion charts for future reference</li>
<li>Document the rationale for your approach for future team members</li>
<li>Review how related languages have handled measurements</li>
<li>Consult published reference Bibles for footnote examples</li>
</ul>

Module 7 (washed but need widgets)
=====
<hr />
<h1>Glossary Linking: Marking Terms in the Text</h1>
<p>
  <strong>Duration:</strong>
  &nbsp;60 minutes
  <br />
  <strong>Level:</strong>
  &nbsp;Experienced Translator
  <br />
  <strong>Goal:</strong>
  &nbsp;Mark glossary terms in the text using the Biblical Terms tool to help readers access definitions and
  explanations
</p>
<hr />
<h2>Introduction</h2>
<p>
  <strong>Time:</strong>
  &nbsp;5 minutes
</p>
<p>
  You've created a helpful glossary defining difficult terms—"atonement," "covenant," "Pharisee," "righteousness." But
  how do readers know which words have glossary entries? How do they find the definition when they encounter an
  unfamiliar term?
</p>
<p>
  In printed Bibles, glossary words are often marked with an asterisk (*) or other symbol. In digital versions, they
  become clickable links. This module teaches you to systematically mark these glossary terms in your text.
</p>
<p>
  <strong>Why wait until the end?</strong>
  &nbsp;Throughout translation, you've made spelling corrections and terminology adjustments. If you marked glossary
  words early, spelling changes would have broken the links. Now that the text is finalized, you can confidently link
  glossary terms without fear of missing occurrences due to spelling variations.
</p>
<h3>What This Module Covers</h3>
<ul>
  <li>Using the Biblical Terms tool to automate glossary linking</li>
  <li>Choosing appropriate linking frequency (first occurrence, all occurrences, etc.)</li>
  <li>Handling glossary entries not in the Biblical Terms list</li>
  <li>Working with phrases and special cases</li>
  <li>Verifying and correcting linking results</li>
  <li>Undoing links when needed</li>
</ul>
<h3>Prerequisites</h3>
<p>Before starting this module, ensure:</p>
<ul>
  <li>Your glossary content is complete and finalized</li>
  <li>You have editing permissions for all books being published</li>
  <li>You're familiar with the Biblical Terms tool</li>
  <li>Your text is substantially finalized (minimal spelling changes expected)</li>
  <li>You understand \w ... \w* markers (word list markers in USFM)</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2>Procedure Walkthrough</h2>
<p>
  <strong>Time:</strong>
  &nbsp;45 minutes
</p>
<h3>Understanding Glossary Linking</h3>
<p><strong>What happens when you link glossary words:</strong></p>
<p>In the text, Paratext wraps glossary words with markers:</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <code>\w word\w*</code>
    &nbsp;for single words
  </li>
  <li>
    <code>\w phrase with multiple words\w*</code>
    &nbsp;for phrases
  </li>
</ul>
<p><strong>In printed publications:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>The typesetter uses these markers to add asterisks or other symbols</li>
  <li>Example: "Jesus taught in the synagogue*" (asterisk points to glossary)</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>In digital publications:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>The markers become clickable links</li>
  <li>Readers tap/click to see the glossary definition</li>
  <li>Links can connect directly to the relevant entry</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Linking frequency choices:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>First occurrence in every section:</strong>
    &nbsp;Mark the term only the first time it appears in each section (most common)
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>First occurrence in every chapter:</strong>
    &nbsp;Mark only the first use in each chapter
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>First occurrence in book:</strong>
    &nbsp;Mark only once per book (minimal)
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>All occurrences:</strong>
    &nbsp;Mark every instance (can clutter the text)
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Strategic principle:</strong>
  &nbsp;Balance helping readers find definitions with not overwhelming the text with markers. "First occurrence in every
  section" usually provides good coverage without clutter.
</p>
<hr />
<h3>Part 1: Link Terms Already in Biblical Terms List</h3>
<p>
  Most glossary terms are likely already in your Biblical Terms list (theological concepts, proper names, key terms).
  These are easiest to link.
</p>
<h4>Step 1: Verify Editing Permissions</h4>
<ol>
  <li>
    Ensure you have editing permission for all books
    <ul>
      <li>Check Project &gt; Permissions</li>
      <li>If limited permissions, contact your project administrator</li>
      <li>You need edit access to insert the \w ... \w* markers</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
</ol>
<h4>Step 2: Open Biblical Terms Tool</h4>
<ol>
  <li>Click in your project window</li>
  <li>
    <strong>≡ Tab</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Tools</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Biblical Terms</strong>
  </li>
</ol>
<h4>Step 3: Select Terms to Link</h4>
<p><strong>Work in small batches:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Don't try to link all glossary terms at once</li>
  <li>Start with 5-10 terms</li>
  <li>Review results before continuing</li>
  <li>This makes it easier to catch and fix problems</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>To select terms:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>In the Biblical Terms window, find terms that have glossary entries</li>
  <li>Hold Ctrl (Windows) or Cmd (Mac) and click to select multiple terms</li>
  <li>Select 5-10 related terms</li>
</ol>
<p>
  <strong>Tip:</strong>
  &nbsp;Start with the most important or frequently occurring terms to learn the process with high-value items.
</p>
<h4>Step 4: Link Renderings to Glossary</h4>
<ol>
  <li>
    With terms selected:&nbsp;
    <strong>≡ Tab</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Edit</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Link Renderings to Glossary</strong>
  </li>
  <li>A dialog box appears with linking options</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>In the dialog:</strong></p>
<p><strong>Linking frequency option:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>
    Choose&nbsp;
    <strong>"First occurrence in every section"</strong>
    &nbsp;(recommended)
  </li>
  <li>Or select another frequency based on your project needs</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Why "first occurrence in every section"?</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Provides regular reminders without cluttering</li>
  <li>Readers who skip to different sections will still see links</li>
  <li>
    <p>Balances helpfulness with readability</p>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p>
      Click&nbsp;
      <strong>OK</strong>
    </p>
  </li>
</ul>
<p><strong>What happens next:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Paratext searches through all your text</li>
  <li>It finds every occurrence of the selected terms</li>
  <li>It adds \w ... \w* markers according to your frequency choice</li>
  <li>A results list shows which verses were changed</li>
</ul>
<h4>Step 5: Review the Results Carefully</h4>
<p>
  <strong>Critical step:</strong>
  &nbsp;Don't assume the linking worked perfectly. Review the results list.
</p>
<p><strong>For each modified verse:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>
    <strong>Click on the verse</strong>
    &nbsp;in the results list to view it in context
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Verify the linking is correct:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Was the right word marked?</li>
      <li>Does the link make sense in this context?</li>
      <li>Were unintended words marked?</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Watch for problems:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Words marked that shouldn't be (false positives)</li>
      <li>Context where the term means something different</li>
      <li>Linking broke across verse boundaries awkwardly</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Common issues to catch:</strong></p>
<p><strong>Homographs (words spelled the same, different meanings):</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Example: "Grace" as a person's name vs. theological concept</li>
  <li>Example: "Lord" referring to God vs. human master</li>
  <li>Solution: Manually remove markers where inappropriate</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Partial word matches:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Example: If linking "man," it might catch "woman" or "human"</li>
  <li>Solution: Review carefully, remove incorrect links</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Awkward phrase boundaries:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Example: Phrase linking might break in unexpected places</li>
  <li>Solution: Adjust manually or unlink and relink differently</li>
</ul>
<h4>Step 6: Make Manual Corrections</h4>
<p>For any inappropriate links:</p>
<ol>
  <li>Navigate to the verse</li>
  <li>Find the incorrectly marked text</li>
  <li>Delete the \w ... \w* markers manually</li>
  <li>Save your changes</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>To remove markers:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>
    Simply delete&nbsp;
    <code>\w</code>
    &nbsp;at the beginning and&nbsp;
    <code>\w*</code>
    &nbsp;at the end
  </li>
  <li>Leave the actual word/phrase text</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h3>Part 2: Link Terms NOT in Biblical Terms List</h3>
<p>
  Some glossary entries may not be in the standard Biblical Terms lists. For these, you need to create custom entries.
</p>
<h4>Step 1: Identify Glossary Terms Not in Biblical Terms</h4>
<p>Review your glossary list:</p>
<ul>
  <li>Which terms don't appear in your Biblical Terms tool?</li>
  <li>These need custom entries</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Common examples:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Cultural terms specific to your translation</li>
  <li>Theological terms not in standard lists</li>
  <li>Local concepts you've explained in the glossary</li>
</ul>
<h4>Step 2: Create Custom Biblical Terms Entries</h4>
<p>
  <strong>
    For detailed instructions on creating Biblical Terms entries, see Module 10.7 of the Paratext training course.
  </strong>
</p>
<p><strong>Basic process:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>
    In Biblical Terms tool:&nbsp;
    <strong>≡ Tab</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Biblical Terms</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Edit Biblical Terms List</strong>
  </li>
  <li>Add a new term entry</li>
  <li>Provide the rendering (how it appears in your text)</li>
  <li>Note it's for glossary linking</li>
  <li>Save the entry</li>
</ol>
<p><strong>Start with a few:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Don't create all custom entries at once</li>
  <li>Add 3-5 glossary terms</li>
  <li>Link them and review results</li>
  <li>Continue with more batches</li>
</ul>
<h4>Step 3: Link Custom Terms</h4>
<p>Once you've created custom Biblical Terms entries:</p>
<ol>
  <li>Select the newly created terms in the Biblical Terms tool</li>
  <li>
    <strong>≡ Tab</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Edit</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Link Renderings to Glossary</strong>
  </li>
  <li>Choose linking frequency</li>
  <li>Review results carefully</li>
</ol>
<hr />
<h3>Part 3: Special Cases and Advanced Tips</h3>
<h4>Working with Phrases</h4>
<p><strong>If you have both a phrase and a word in your glossary:</strong></p>
<p>Example: Both "Holy Spirit" (phrase) and "Spirit" (word)</p>
<p>
  <strong>Best practice:</strong>
  &nbsp;Link the longer phrase first
</p>
<ol>
  <li>Link "Holy Spirit" first</li>
  <li>Then link "Spirit"</li>
  <li>This prevents "Spirit" from being linked inside "Holy Spirit" incorrectly</li>
</ol>
<h4>Renderings with Asterisks</h4>
<p><strong>If your rendering already contains "*" (asterisk):</strong></p>
<p>Example: Your glossary entry for a term uses "*" for some reason</p>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;The asterisk might interfere with glossary markers
</p>
<p><strong>Solution:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>Complete the linking process</li>
  <li>Carefully review all results</li>
  <li>Manually remove \w ... \w* markers where the asterisk creates confusion</li>
  <li>Consider whether the asterisk is necessary in the rendering</li>
</ol>
<h4>Terms with Multiple Renderings</h4>
<p><strong>If a term has very different renderings in different contexts:</strong></p>
<p>Example: "Spirit" might be "Holy Spirit" or "evil spirit"</p>
<p><strong>Two options:</strong></p>
<p>
  <strong>Option 1:</strong>
  &nbsp;Create separate Biblical Terms entries
</p>
<ul>
  <li>One for "Holy Spirit"</li>
  <li>One for "spirit" (general)</li>
  <li>Link each appropriately with its own glossary entry</li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Option 2:</strong>
  &nbsp;Link selectively
</p>
<ul>
  <li>Use automatic linking for one rendering</li>
  <li>Manually add/remove markers for the other contexts</li>
</ul>
<h4>Homograph Challenges</h4>
<p><strong>If a Biblical Term rendering is identical to another term:</strong></p>
<p>Example: "Grace" as both a name and a theological concept</p>
<p><strong>Solution:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>Link the term</li>
  <li>Carefully review ALL results</li>
  <li>Manually remove markers where it's the wrong meaning</li>
  <li>This requires careful attention but ensures accuracy</li>
</ol>
<p>
  <strong>Consider:</strong>
  &nbsp;Adding a brief note in the glossary distinguishing the uses if confusion is likely.
</p>
<hr />
<h3>Part 4: Verify and Finalize</h3>
<h4>Review Complete Linking</h4>
<p>After linking all glossary terms:</p>
<ol>
  <li>
    <p>
      <strong>Scan through your text</strong>
      &nbsp;in different books
    </p>
    <ul>
      <li>Are glossary words appropriately marked?</li>
      <li>Is the frequency appropriate (not too many, not too few)?</li>
      <li>Are there obvious terms that should be linked but aren't?</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Check your glossary list</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Have all glossary entries been linked in the text?</li>
      <li>Are there glossary entries without corresponding text links?</li>
      <li>Should any glossary entries be removed (not actually used)?</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Test in different views</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Preview view: Can you see the markers?</li>
      <li>Formatted view: Do markers render correctly?</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
</ol>
<h4>Undo Links if Needed</h4>
<p><strong>If you have many unwanted results from a linking operation:</strong></p>
<ol>
  <li>Select the same terms in Biblical Terms tool</li>
  <li>
    <strong>≡ Tab</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Edit</strong>
    &nbsp;&gt;&nbsp;
    <strong>Unlink Renderings to Glossary</strong>
  </li>
  <li>This removes \w ... \w* markers for those terms</li>
  <li>You can then retry with different settings or manual approach</li>
</ol>
<p>
  <strong>This is reversible:</strong>
  &nbsp;Don't panic if a batch linking goes wrong—just unlink and try again.
</p>
<h4>Coordinate with Typesetter</h4>
<p>Before finalizing:</p>
<ol>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Ask your typesetter:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>How should glossary links appear in print? (asterisk, superscript letter, other?)</li>
      <li>Are there any special requirements for the markers?</li>
      <li>Do they need a list of linked terms?</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Provide documentation:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>List of all glossary terms</li>
      <li>Explanation of how you've marked them</li>
      <li>Any special cases they should be aware of</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
</ol>
<hr />
<h2>Verification Checklist</h2>
<p>
  <strong>Time:</strong>
  &nbsp;5 minutes
</p>
<p>After completing this module, verify that you have:</p>
<p><strong>Preparation:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] Verified editing permissions for all books</li>
  <li>[ ] Glossary content is finalized</li>
  <li>[ ] Identified which terms need linking</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Linking Process:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] Worked through glossary terms in small batches (5-10 at a time)</li>
  <li>[ ] Used "Link Renderings to Glossary" function</li>
  <li>[ ] Chose appropriate linking frequency (usually "first occurrence in every section")</li>
  <li>[ ] Reviewed results list for each batch</li>
  <li>[ ] Made manual corrections for inappropriate links</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Special Cases:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] Linked longer phrases before shorter words where both exist</li>
  <li>[ ] Created custom Biblical Terms entries for glossary terms not in standard lists</li>
  <li>[ ] Reviewed and corrected homograph issues</li>
  <li>[ ] Addressed any rendering-specific problems (asterisks, etc.)</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Quality Verification:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] Scanned through text to verify appropriate marking</li>
  <li>[ ] Checked that all glossary entries are linked in text</li>
  <li>[ ] Removed inappropriate links (wrong context, wrong meaning)</li>
  <li>[ ] Verified linking frequency is appropriate (not too much/little)</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Coordination:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] Contacted typesetter about marker requirements</li>
  <li>[ ] Provided list of linked terms</li>
  <li>[ ] Documented any special cases</li>
  <li>[ ] Team has reviewed and approved linking approach</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Final Status:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>[ ] All glossary terms appropriately linked in text</li>
  <li>[ ] No unintended or incorrect links</li>
  <li>[ ] Linking frequency supports reader usability</li>
  <li>[ ] Typesetter has necessary information</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2>Troubleshooting</h2>
<p>
  <strong>Time:</strong>
  &nbsp;3 minutes
</p>
<h3>Common Issues and Solutions</h3>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Link Renderings to Glossary function is greyed out/unavailable"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Ensure: (1) You have terms selected in Biblical Terms tool, (2) You have editing permissions, (3) Your project
    is not locked for editing. If still unavailable, contact Language Technology Consultant.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Linking marked words I don't want linked"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;This is normal with automatic linking. Review results list carefully, manually remove unwanted \w ... \w*
    markers. Consider if rendering is too broad or matches unintended words.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"A glossary term isn't being linked anywhere"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Check: (1) Is the term actually in your text with that exact spelling? (2) Is it in your Biblical Terms list?
    (3) Does the rendering match exactly? Use Find to search for the term in your text.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Linking added markers across verse boundaries awkwardly"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Manually adjust the markers. Move the \w and \w* to appropriate boundaries within single verses. This may
    require breaking a phrase link or reformatting.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"I linked everything and now my text is cluttered with markers"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;You may have chosen "all occurrences" instead of "first occurrence in section." Unlink and redo with "first
    occurrence in every section" for better balance.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Custom Biblical Terms entry I created isn't working for linking"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Verify: (1) The rendering exactly matches how it appears in text, (2) The entry is saved properly, (3) You've
    selected it before using Link Renderings. Try creating the entry again if problems persist.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Some links work in some books but not others"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Check: (1) Do you have editing permission for all books? (2) Are spelling variations between books? (3) Run
    linking separately for problem books to identify the issue.
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Problem:</strong>
  &nbsp;"Typesetter says they can't see the glossary markers"
</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Solution:</strong>
    &nbsp;Ensure you've saved and sent your project. Markers use \w ... \w* which are standard USFM. If typesetter still
    can't see them, ask Language Technology Consultant to verify markers are correct.
  </li>
</ul>
<h3>When to Ask for Help</h3>
<p><strong>Contact your Language Technology Consultant if:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>Link Renderings to Glossary function isn't working (technical issue)</li>
  <li>Markers aren't appearing correctly in different views (display problem)</li>
  <li>You need help creating custom Biblical Terms entries (tool usage)</li>
  <li>Unlink function isn't removing markers (software problem)</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Contact your Translation Consultant if:</strong></p>
<ul>
  <li>You're unsure which terms should be in the glossary (content decision)</li>
  <li>You need guidance on linking frequency (publishing standards)</li>
  <li>You're uncertain about handling homographs (translation issue)</li>
  <li>You need approval for custom Biblical Terms entries (project oversight)</li>
</ul>
<hr />
<h2>Summary and Next Steps</h2>
<p>
  <strong>Time:</strong>
  &nbsp;2 minutes
</p>
<h3>Key Takeaways</h3>
<ul>
  <li>Glossary linking should be done near the end when text is finalized</li>
  <li>The Biblical Terms tool automates linking using "Link Renderings to Glossary"</li>
  <li>"First occurrence in every section" provides good balance of helpfulness without clutter</li>
  <li>Always review results carefully—automatic linking isn't perfect</li>
  <li>Work in small batches (5-10 terms at a time) for easier management</li>
  <li>Longer phrases should be linked before shorter words to avoid conflicts</li>
  <li>Custom Biblical Terms entries may be needed for glossary terms not in standard lists</li>
  <li>Undo/unlink is available if you need to redo linking operations</li>
</ul>

<p>
  <strong>Congratulations!</strong>
  &nbsp;You've completed all the finalizing for publication modules. Your next steps:
</p>
<ol>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Final comprehensive review:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Review all modules' verification checklists</li>
      <li>Confirm all tasks are complete</li>
      <li>Address any remaining items</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Consultant approval:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Submit complete project to Translation Consultant</li>
      <li>Address any final feedback</li>
      <li>Obtain sign-off for publication</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Typesetter coordination:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Provide all necessary files and documentation</li>
      <li>Communicate any special requirements</li>
      <li>Review typeset samples</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
  <li>
    <p><strong>Publication process:</strong></p>
    <ul>
      <li>Follow your organization's publication workflow</li>
      <li>Maintain archival copies</li>
      <li>Celebrate the completion of this major milestone!</li>
    </ul>
  </li>
</ol>
<h3>Timeline Guidance</h3>
<p>Glossary linking typically takes:</p>
<ul>
  <li>
    <strong>Planning and preparation:</strong>
    &nbsp;1 hour
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>Linking terms in batches:</strong>
    &nbsp;3-5 hours (depending on number of terms)
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>Review and corrections:</strong>
    &nbsp;2-4 hours
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>Creating custom entries:</strong>
    &nbsp;1-2 hours (if needed)
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>Final verification:</strong>
    &nbsp;1 hour
  </li>
  <li>
    <strong>Total:</strong>
    &nbsp;8-13 hours spread over several work sessions
  </li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Best approach:</strong>
  &nbsp;Work in focused sessions
</p>
<ul>
  <li>Link 5-10 terms</li>
  <li>Review results immediately</li>
  <li>Make corrections</li>
  <li>Take a break</li>
  <li>Continue with next batch</li>
</ul>
<p>
  <strong>Don't rush:</strong>
  &nbsp;Glossary links are visible to readers. Quality matters more than speed.
</p>
